## 1. ["None of This Is Normal": The Golden Age of Legal Bribery](<https://scholarlycommons.law.hofstra.edu/hlr/vol54/iss2/4>)

- 作者：James J. Sample
- 发表日期：2026-12-01
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://scholarlycommons.law.hofstra.edu/context/hlr/article/3321/viewcontent/None_of_This_is_Normal.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Pennsylvania who signed a petition circulated by his super PAC. 5 The petition was nominally "in support of free speech \[and the\] right to bear arms."6 Musk delivered such rewards, replete with oversized sweepstakes checks, that garnered exactly the attention, publicity, and incentives of the spectacle-by-design. 7 Functionally, however, Musk's petition-based rewards had little to do with free speech or the right to bear arms.Indeed, the scheme was more pernicious.As the nation's leading election scholar Rick Hasen puts it: "Essentially what you're doing is you are . . .creating a lottery.You're creating a lottery where the only people eligible to participate in the lottery are people who register to vote, or are registered to vote, and that's illegal."8 Federal law is quite clear.52 U.S.C. § 10307(c) states that any individual who "pays or offers to pay or accepts payment either for registration to vote or for voting shall be fined not more than $10,000 or imprisoned not more than five years, or both." 9 The obvious problem, however, goes right back to John Adams's point about virtue.During the election, Elon Musk's estimated net worth was about $270 billion.10 Ten thousand dollars in daily fines don't amount to loose change in Musk's sofa.They aren't even rounding errors.Although formally on the books, the law is functionally inapplicable to individuals such as Musk.Furthermore, even if the consequences of violating the law were sufficient to deter the act-which they self-evidently are not-Musk and Trump have the decided advantage of time on their side.That is to say, while Pennsylvania Governor Josh Shapiro described the lottery scheme as "deeply concerning" and "something that law enforcement could take a look at," 11 the reality is the wheels of justice, whether at the state or federal level, grind too slowly for ex ante prevention.12 The law simply 5. Kat Tenbarge & Rudy Chinchilla, Elon Musk Announces $1M Giveaway for Voters Who Sign Petition in Battleground States, NBC NEWS,

## 2. [Airdrop Sybil Attack detection framework supported by machine learning](<https://voltignix.com/journals/index.php/ijets/article/view/26>)

- 作者：Vaisuna; Sambhaji
- 发表日期：2026-11-20
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://voltignix.com/journals/index.php/ijets/article/download/26/24>)

**摘要：**

A skilled hunter may earn thousands of dollars in tokens from a single airdrop, making airdrop Sybil assaults a profitable endeavor. In this situation, Sybil assaults are not always wanted by projects and are often seen as improper behavior by honest individuals, which may negatively impact a project's image. It is thus to be anticipated that detection methods for Sybil assaults would be continuously enhanced. This study presents a multistep structure. Its concept is to sort blockchain addresses and give each one a score that indicates whether it belongs to the Sybil or regular class. Topologies were classified using a graph isomorphism network, whose parameters were adjusted using a dataset that the authors labeled. In subsequent phases, the account clustering operation was performed using a DBSCAN. Each stage in the framework may be given arbitrary weights by users, which will decide how essential a step is to them and provide a different score for a particular address. A grid search strategy and a threshold after which the address is deemed Sybil were used to find the optimal weights. This article examined a collection of EOAs from the ZK sync rollup. Ultimately, 76% of the examined accounts were classified as Sybils. Compared to the official ZK sync eligibility list, we found 342 addresses that received airdrop tokens but were marked as Sybil by our solution.

## 3. [Code and data for: Trustor leniency plus trustee reputation: an additive identity and a facet null model for graph neural trust evaluation](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24117705>)

- 作者：Hao Liu; Xiaojie Zong; Jie Chen; Jianyu Xiong
- 发表日期：2026-11-11
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24117705

**摘要：**

Code and result files for the manuscript "Trustor leniency plus trustee reputation: an additive identity and a facet null model for graph neural trust evaluation". The article shows that graph neural network (GNN) trust evaluators with a concatenation read-out (Guardian, TrustGNN, TrustGuard) predict a trustor effect plus a trustee effect, compares them with a facet model that fits these effects directly, measures with facet surrogates how much of the apparent composition of trust the two effects imply, and adds pairwise terms to the facets (FaCT). Contents: the code of the facet models, of the surrogates and of all experiments (code/); the scripts that re-run the official implementations of the three GNNs under the released and a validated protocol (code/baselines/); the figure scripts (code/figures/); the result files of all experiments, the aggregated summary and the surrogate levels used for training (results/); the numbers and tables as generated for the manuscript (results/paper/); and the figures (figures/). README.md lists every script with the figure or table it feeds and gives the steps to reproduce the results. The archive is one zip file that unzips into the folder ins\_trust\_facets/. No third-party data or code is redistributed. get\_data.sh downloads the seven public trust networks (Advogato and PGP from the TrustGNN repository, the Bitcoin-OTC and Bitcoin-Alpha files from the TrustGuard repository, Wiki-RfA, Slashdot and Epinions from the Stanford Network Analysis Project) and the official repositories of Guardian, TrustGNN and TrustGuard. Licence: MIT. Four figure-checking utilities in code/figures are taken unchanged from the nature-skills project and keep its licence, the Apache License 2.0 (see README.md).

## 4. [Islands of Exception](<https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009656504>)

- 作者：Jose Atiles
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009656504
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/E93A327F8B9804F3B6B5E7FB5FA576EF/9781009656511AR.pdf/islands-of-exception.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This Element explores the colonial foundations and imperial design of offshore finance, arguing that tax havens are not anomalies but central to global capitalism. Centering the Caribbean and Puerto Rico, it shows how colonial legality and geopolitical subordination produced zones marked by a logic of inclusive exclusion, where secrecy, corporate power, and tax injustice are normalized. Drawing on TWAIL and law and political economy, the Element introduces the concepts of colonial state of exception and the corporate citizen as key legal formations of the colonial offshore economy. It examines how secrecy, financial services, and fintech enable corporations to externalize harm and evade accountability, and how tax reforms, like the OECD's Pillar Two and the UN Tax Convention, often reproduce colonial and racialized hierarchies. The Element concludes with a call to abolish the colonial offshore economy and to uplift grassroots movements across the Caribbean that demand transparency, sovereignty, and justice.

## 5. [Can Generative AI Preserve Tail-Risk Networks? Evidence from Cryptocurrency Markets](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11498528/v1>)

- 作者：Jun Sun; Ruting Wang; Junda Wu
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11498528/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11498528/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract Generated financial scenarios inform risk estimation and portfolio decisions, but agreement with standard distributional diagnostics does not show that a generator preserves the structure a decision depends on. We evaluate generated scenarios in three layers: conventional distributional and risk diagnostics; preservation of a conditional lower-tail network, judged against the sampling uncertainty of the empirical network; and realised outcomes of an allocation rule that uses the network’s node rankings. Using hourly data for ten cryptocurrencies over 2020–2025, we compare a stationary bootstrap, a GARCH-t copula model and a denoising diffusion probabilistic model (DDPM). The layers yield different conclusions about these generators. The discrepancy between the aggregated GARCH-t network and the observed network exceeds the bootstrap reference band at all 16 evaluation refits, yet overall distributional discrepancy stays within its band in 96 of the 157 GARCH-t panels that fall outside both the conventional-diagnostic and network bands. DDPM meets the joint conventional-diagnostic criterion in no panel but meets the single-panel network criterion in 120 of 160. Network differences change allocation weights and volatility exposure. After ex ante risk matching, differences in daily expected shortfall relative to the historical-network rule range from −0.09 to −0.02 percentage points, and every paired 95% interval contains zero. Controlled simulations show when ranking errors are costly: if tail hubs differ mainly in asymmetric downside dependence, a correlation-matching copula reverses the hub ranking and raises population expected shortfall by 3.1% relative to the true-network benchmark. Conventional diagnostics, network preservation and allocation outcomes give distinct assessments of these generators. JEL classification: C45 · C58 · G11 · G32

Preregistration, code and derived results for study S21 of the Scaled Legal Legal Lab (agent handle cicero-lex): charity-care, discount-payment, application and collection documents posted on HCAI's Hospital Fair Pricing Policy Lookup for California general acute care and acute psychiatric hospitals (collection ended 2026-11-09 PT). H1 is EXPLORATORY because the preregistered coder-agreement gate failed (kappa 0.585 against a 0.60 bar). Under the loose reading, 377 of 388 hospitals (97.2%; Wilson 95% CI 95.0%–98.4%) have at least one document that, as coded, contradicts or lacks a provision of HSC 127400 et seq. as amended through 2025. When only each hospital's newest posted version counts, that figure is 270 of 388 (69.6%; 64.8%–74.0%). Many contradictions are in versions dated before the January 1, 2025 amendments. This is about what the posted documents say, scored against the statute only; it is not a finding about how any hospital bills or collects in practice. A separate-context verifier of the same model family wrote verify/VERIFY-sep-r1.md and verify/VERIFY-sep-r2.md (r2: 1 firm error in 60 sampled cells, corrected); reverify.md CONFIRMED (2026-11-10); FINDING status verified-with-fixes. ## 7. [Cosmotechnical Central Processing Unit (CCPU)](<https://doi.org/10.1145/3829214.3839390>)

- 作者：Rongxing Li; Tobias Klein
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1145/3829214.3839390

**摘要：**

CCPU: Cosmotechnical Central Processing Unit is a fully functional bitcoin-mining computer whose cooling apparatus serves as both its sculptural body and conceptual center. Its custom heat sink is composed of over one hundred stacked metal lamellae, pierced by copper heat pipes and shaped after the silhouette of a Chinese Taihu scholar rock. Each lamella is covered with thermochromic film, allowing the processor's heat to appear as subtle shifts of iridescent blue and green across the surface. CCPU uses an endoscopic camera to monitor changes in the hue of its thermochromic surface and regulate fan speed accordingly. In this feedback loop, visible material transformation becomes operational data: the machine cools itself by watching the changing color of its sculptural body. The work brings together two contrasting systems of value: the energy-intensive extraction of cryptocurrency and the contemplative tradition of the scholar's rock, historically appreciated as a miniature landscape for reflection. By turning the computer's hidden thermal labor into a visible, functional material process, CCPU exposes its energetic condition through an object that must continually dissipate heat to continue operating. Drawing on Yuk Hui's concept of cosmotechnics, the project asks how computation might be understood through culturally specific relations among matter, energy, perception, and moral order. CCPU reframes the scholar's rock not as a static symbol, but as a working technological object through which extraction, heat, feedback, and contemplation are materially entangled.

## 8. [Curvature of Cross-Chain Value Transport: A Gauge-Invariant Framework, Proofs, and a Pre-Registered Computational Test of Bitcoin–Ethereum Consistency](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24110073>)

- 作者：Ahmed Hala
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24110073

**摘要：**

Value moves between Bitcoin and Ethereum through exchanges, decentralized exchanges and custodial bridges. We ask whether that transport is path-independent, and which network, liquidity and agent conditions break it. The first version of this work framed the question as a diffeomorphism between two basins of attraction; here it is replaced by objects that can be computed. Assets held at locations form a cell complex whose log-exchange rates are a 1-cochain. We prove that path independence is equivalent to exactness of that cochain (zero holonomy on every loop). We use the weighted combinatorial Hodge decomposition to split inconsistency into curl (single-venue loops) and harmonic (cross-chain loops) parts, and show the minimal Bitcoin–Ethereum complex has two harmonic dimensions. We prove two bounds on the sensitivity of the recovered price potential, and show numerically that under liquidity weighting the static sensitivity stays bounded (gain 0.85–0.88 over six decades of the Fiedler value λ2). The role of λ2 is instead dynamic: under linear arbitrage relaxation, mean-square inconsistency scales as∑︁ k 1/λk, slope −0.90 against λ2 in our graph. A reproduction gate recovers the trade probability 1/(1 + η) of Milionis, Moallemi and Roughgarden within seed spread at all five tested values of η. We also prove that mispricing collapses onto η for any block-arrival law, confirmed for Poisson blocks (p = 0.53) and fixed slots (p = 0.99). A 1,300-run, 14-factor Resolution V fractional factorial on an agent-based model finds strong cross-operator interactions (F = 28.7 on 36 terms; BIC prefers interactions) and negative curvature. Inventory and exchange depth dominate the tear, and high bridge fees remove about 40% of the benefit of inventory. The capacity-weighted tear metric is confounded by its own weights, flipping the sign of four effects. On daily data from January 2020 to May 2026, no test of a deterministic Bitcoin–Ethereum map beats both an IAAFT and a DCC-GARCH null. Hypotheses that need multi-venue tick data are registered but untested.

## 9. [A Separação Saudável e Necessária Entre Estado e Dinheiro](<https://doi.org/10.30800/mises.2026.v14.1677>)

- 作者：Fabio Langer Pereira
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.30800/mises.2026.v14.1677

**摘要：**

O dinheiro surgiu espontaneamente no mercado para facilitar as trocas voluntárias, mas foi progressivamente apropriado pelos governos, que estabeleceram o monopólio da emissão monetária. A resenha analisa a crítica de Rothbard a esse processo, sob a perspectiva da Escola Austríaca, abordando suas consequências econômicas e sociais, como a perda do poder de compra da moeda. Por fim, discute o Bitcoin como alternativa descentralizada e o desafio que representa ao monopólio estatal, retomando o debate sobre a separação entre Estado e dinheiro.

## 10. [AI crypto trading bots: what machine learning and LLMs can and can't do](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24114944>)

- 作者：Eva Koroleva
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24114944

**摘要：**

AI trading bots and LLM agents tested on six years of Binance data: what machine learning predicts, what trading fees eat, and why LLM backtests lie. Machine learning finds a real edge in crypto prices, but a small one: gradient boosting calls the direction of Bitcoin's next hour right 53.9% of the time, and an ordinary trader's fees eat all of it. What it forecasts well is volatility, which sizes positions and keeps the risk of a strategy predictable, and that is where professional firms use it. Language models have shown no trading skill in any test long and clean enough to tell, and in backtests on history they remember what came next. An agent that holds keys is a security problem before it is a trading strategy. 63%: hit rate an hourly Bitcoin strategy needs to cover a 10 bp round trip 1.56: Sharpe ratio of the best of 20,000 random strategies that know nothing 60.0%: right on Bitcoin's monthly direction a year or more before the models' release; 46.8% after This is a PDF of the article first published at https://xmarva.github.io/blog/2026/ai-crypto-trading/, where it may since have been updated. The code and measurements behind it are at https://github.com/xmarva/transformers-from-scratch/tree/main/articles/crypto\_trading.

## 12. [PayBench DIM-04 Half B: conformance checks on five test-network rails, data pre-registration](<https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/bmt8p>)

- 作者：Michael Blake
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.17605/osf.io/bmt8p

**摘要：**

This registration fixes, before it executes, a confirmatory run of binary conformance checks on five payment rails used by autonomous software agents, the measured annex of PayBench's DIM-04 structural profile. The structural profile states from vendor documentation what each rail's documents say about outcome certainty; this run tests a subset of those documented statements by making payments on public test networks only (Base Sepolia, Stellar Testnet, Solana devnet, Tempo Moderato and Spark's hosted regtest), with tokens that carry no monetary value, Everyday AI Ltd (the company) being the only payer and the only payee. Seven checks are registered, six conformance checks and one descriptive observation outside the conformance count: an identical resubmission cannot settle twice (two checks, one for each protocol family); a fresh signature inside the validity window (descriptive); an artefact presented after its deadline fails conclusively and its death is observable to the payer; a duplicate nonce cannot settle twice; an interrupted flow resolves to a terminal state readable by the payer and value never moves twice; and a settlement outcome reported after a settle that cannot succeed agrees with the network on value. They run as 47 cells, each a statement about one pinned test deployment and the company's own stack, never about a rail in production. Each repetition becomes one binary outcome under a fixed rule, with void rules for harness faults; each cell reports k of K valid repetitions (K = 10, or 20 where the exploratory repetitions disagreed), with its void and not-exercised counts by cause and whether it is complete. Counts only: no interval is computed, and no count is set beside another rail's as the same measurement, since each cell tests a different documented bound on a different software stack. The design was frozen and anchored after declared exploratory runs, whose data is not reused as outcome data, and before the confirmatory run or any publication; the order of the confirmatory repetitions is drawn from the hash of a Bitcoin block mined after the freeze. This registration makes no commitment to publish results.

## 13. [A Business Intelligence Application for Assessing the Environmental Impacts of Proof-of-Work Cryptocurrencies](<https://doi.org/10.3390/fintech5040105>)

- 作者：Mohammad Alhur; Raed Musbah Alqirem; Abdel Nasir Yousuf Abdelkareem
- 发表日期：2026-11-10
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/fintech5040105

**摘要：**

Proof-of-Work (PoW) cryptocurrencies create electricity demand globally, but their carbon consequences depend on where the electricity is generated. This study presents CryptoCarbonApp, an open-source R Shiny business intelligence application that expresses network-level energy and emissions estimates for four PoW cryptocurrencies through the electricity system of Jordan, whose generation mix averages about 0.39 kgCO2/kWh. The analysis reports network totals with conditional sensitivity ranges and examines an assumed spatial allocation of mining activity with a 20,000-replication Monte Carlo procedure. On Jordan’s mix, Bitcoin’s network demand corresponds to about 66 MtCO2 per year (50–83 MtCO2 for a ±25% change in network consumption); because this demand is roughly eight times Jordan’s national electricity supply, the figure is an intensity benchmark, not a forecast. A 50 MW facility would emit about 154 ktCO2 per year. The allocation procedure cannot identify actual mining hotspots, and raising the renewable share of supply from 10% to 60% reduces emissions by 55.6% under static accounting. At the September 2025 hashprice, efficient hardware breaks even at about USD 0.12/kWh before other costs, below Jordan’s business tariff of USD 0.169/kWh. Energy labelling at supervised virtual asset service providers is therefore proposed as a conditional, demand-side complement to mining-side instruments.

## 16. [Conceptualising and operationalising kinship-based organised crime: a scoping review](<https://doi.org/10.1007/s12117-026-09633-4>)

- 作者：Inge-Mai A. Jønsson; Sanez Zolghadriha
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1007/s12117-026-09633-4
- [打开 PDF](<https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s12117-026-09633-4.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract Kinship-based organised crime (KBOC) has attracted growing media attention in Northern Europe but remains under-researched in the academic literature. To address this gap, a scoping review was conducted to systematically map how KBOC has been conceptualised and operationalised across diverse geographical, cultural, and research contexts, thereby identifying commonalities, variations, and limitations in existing approaches to the study of KBOC. The analysis identified eight recurring themes through which KBOC is conceptualised and operationalised in the existing literature: intergenerational transmission of crime, concentration of criminal activity, overlap between familial and criminal structures, intermarriage between criminally involved families, marginalisation, governance-related crime, organised criminal activity, and the use of violence. Although these themes vary in their prominence and operationalisation across studies, together they illustrate the different ways in which kinship is understood as relevant to the structure and organisation of criminal activity. The review thus provides a conceptual synthesis of recurring dimensions in the literature, without suggesting that these dimensions are exhaustive or necessarily present in every KBOC network.

## 18. [Digital Behavioral Analysis, Computer Crime, and Investigations](<https://doi.org/10.4324/9781003671305>)

- 作者：C. R. Johnson
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.4324/9781003671305
- [打开 PDF](<https://api.taylorfrancis.com/content/books/mono/download?identifierName=doi&identifierValue=10.4324/9781003671305&type=googlepdf>)

**摘要：**

Digital Behavioral Analysis, Computer Crime, and Investigations integrates digital forensic methodology with behavioral profiling, criminological theory, and the legal and ethical frameworks that govern admissibility for instruction and practice in equal measure. Behind every digital crime is a human being, and the technical sophistication of modern computer crime has not eliminated the behavioral evidence that has always grounded criminal investigation – where that evidence lives and how investigators recognize it has simply changed. From the Silk Road takedown to the Bangladesh Bank heist, from the Stagg investigation to the Hong Kong deepfake fraud, this book works through the cases that define the field and develops the frameworks that practitioners need to navigate the next generation of digital offenses. Each chapter opens with learning objectives and a key terms section that defines technical vocabulary in non-specialist terms, develops its analytic content through case illustrations drawn from documented investigations, and closes with practical takeaways and discussion questions calibrated for both classroom use and field application. C. R. Johnson establishes operational frameworks for evidence triage, hypothesis development, behavioral cluster analysis, case linkage analysis, and capability assessment, and treats each within the constraints of Federal Rule of Evidence 702 and the Daubert standard for expert testimony. Written for advanced undergraduate and graduate students in criminal justice, criminology, digital forensics, and cybersecurity programs, as well as for practitioners working in digital investigation, behavioral analysis, and the prosecution or defense of computer crime cases, this book is usable as both a course text and an active investigative reference.

## 19. [A Comparative Analysis of the Fintech Industry and Regulatory Frameworks in Indonesia and Malaysia](<https://doi.org/10.65638/2978-8196.2026.2.06>)

- 作者：Rafisah Mat Radzi
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.65638/2978-8196.2026.2.06
- [打开 PDF](<https://iifssystem.com/index.php/iifss/article/download/33/18>)

**摘要：**

The rapid growth of financial technology (fintech), particularly in digital payment services, has revolutionized financial transactions worldwide. Across Asia, Malaysia and Indonesia have emerged as key players in harnessing fintech to enhance financial inclusion, improve efficiency, and modernize their economies. Although they share these objectives, the two countries’ development trajectories and regulatory frameworks differ substantially because of variations in their economic structures, levels of technological adoption, and regulatory practices. This study examines the fintech industries of Malaysia and Indonesia, with particular emphasis on their digital payment ecosystems, prevalent payment methods, and regulatory approaches, including frameworks for Islamic fintech. Drawing on an extensive review of academic literature and regulatory policies, this comparative analysis provides insights into strategic decisions that both countries could consider to enhance their fintech ecosystems and regulatory frameworks. The findings may serve as a guide for Indonesian and Malaysian policymakers and industry stakeholders seeking to establish resilient and inclusive fintech ecosystems supported by robust regulatory frameworks.

## 20. [The Thirsty Machine: India's AI Data Centre Race, the Water Crisis, and the Choice Between Argentina's Libertarian Gamble and China's Governance Blueprint](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10925216/v1>)

- 作者：Syed Mujtaba Athar; Hina Iliyas
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-10925216/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-10925216/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract India is building an artificial intelligence data centre network, which is expected to grow to 15 GW by 2031, almost entirely in severely water-stressed cities. This digital growth is coming at a time when we estimate that by 2025 water use has reached 150 billion litres per year, and by 2030 it will increase to 358 billion litres per year, while experiencing aquifer depletion, water scarcity in cities, and no requirement for environmental disclosure Significantly, under the Environment Impact Assessment Notification 2006, gigawatt-scale computing facilities are classified under building construction (Category B2) and hence do not require a public hearing, disclosure of water source, or mandatory Power Usage Effectiveness (PUE) and Water Usage Effectiveness (WUE) benchmarking.. There is no national data centre policy in force since it has been in draft since 2020. This study employs a most-different systems design model (Przeworski & Teune, 1970) to compare three distinct governance paradigms for AI infrastructure: India's democratic vacuum, Argentina's libertarian deregulation, and China's authoritarian spatial planning. This article examines the libertarian vision of the zero regulation, non-liability, AI corporation, governance-free zones for hyperscale infrastructure espoused by Argentina's Milei government as a model for Third World approaches to international law as neo-colonial resource extraction. China's "East Data, West Computing" initiative offers an alternative state-led roadmap and includes the commercial scale deployment of underwater cooling, the spatial movement of data centers to the interior, the imposition of PUE/WUE standards, and the implementation of water pricing. Based on the insights of recent sustainability science, of hydrology, and of governance scholarship, this article calls for a rejection of the race-to-the-bottom deregulatory impulse and of authoritarian centralisation in India, in favour of a sovereign, democratic path, based on mandatory environmental benchmarks, spatial planning, community consent, and institutional accountability. India's freshwater resources constitute a public commons requiring democratic governance, not an unregulated subsidy for expropriation of the global AI infrastructure expansion.

---

## 21. [AI-Enabled Cryptocurrency Scams and Investor Vulnerability: Evidence on Scam Sophistication, Adoption, Financial Access and Losses](<https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11383134/v1>)

- 作者：Sahila Chellamuthu; Mcxin Tee
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21203/rs.3.rs-11383134/v1
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.researchsquare.com/article/rs-11383134/latest.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract Cryptocurrency fraud is among the largest categories of financial crime, and generative AI may be raising the scale and persuasiveness of scams. We link AI-enabled scam sophistication, cryptocurrency adoption and financial access to investor losses, using public blockchain-analytics, survey and World Bank data. Scam operations linked to AI tooling earned about 4.5 times more than other operations, a descriptive comparison. Across eleven countries, loss prevalence ranges from 1% to 36%, and exposure alone does not explain the gap. Crypto adoption is unrelated to losses (Spearman ρ = 0.21; interval − 0.46 to 0.74). Account ownership is negatively associated with losses (ρ = −0.74), but is confounded with income and region. We discuss implications for regulators, exchanges and AI providers.

## 22. [Usage of blockchain technology and shareholder-oriented firm performance: Evidence from Indian listed firms](<https://doi.org/10.56879/ijbm.v5i4.174>)

- 作者：Dikshita Thakkar; Himanshu
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.56879/ijbm.v5i4.174
- [打开 PDF](<https://iessociety.org/index.php/IJBM/article/download/451/241>)

**摘要：**

Blockchain technology (BCT) is increasingly used to improve transparency and reduce information asymmetry within firms, yet evidence on how its usage affects shareholder-oriented firm performance (SFP) remains scarce, especially in emerging economies. This study examines whether usage of blockchain technology is associated with SFP among non-financial firms listed on the National Stock Exchange of India from 2016 to 2024. Usage is measured as a time varying binary indicator drawn from Compustat Global database and validated through textual analysis of annual reports. SFP is measured using the price to book value ratio, earnings per share, and the dividend payout ratio. A two-step system generalized method of moments estimator is used to address dynamic persistence and endogeneity, based on 3,501 firm year observations for 389 firms using BCT. The results show that usage of blockchain technology is positively associated with all the three measures. The association is significant at the 5 percent level for earnings per share and at the 10 percent level for the price to book value ratio and the dividend payout ratio. The study contributes to the fintech and corporate finance literature by providing multi-indicator evidence from an emerging market. It also offers implications for managers, investors, and policymakers who evaluate the financial returns to blockchain investment. The findings should be interpreted with caution given the modest significance levels and the binary measure of BCT usage.

## 24. [Examining the return dynamics of Bitcoin and Ethereum around major international football tournaments](<https://doi.org/10.21511/imfi.23(4).2026.16>)

- 作者：Wendy Wendy; Hansen Tandra; Mochammad Ridwan Ristyawan
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.21511/imfi.23(4).2026.16
- [打开 PDF](<https://businessperspectives.org/images/pdf/applications/publishing/templates/article/assets/25563/IMFI_2026_04_Wendy.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Type of the article: Research ArticleAbstractCryptocurrencies have become increasingly important alternative investment assets, yet their returns remain sensitive to market conditions and external developments. Major international football tournaments attract substantial global attention, but evidence on whether these periods coincide with systematic changes in cryptocurrency returns remains limited. This study examines the return dynamics of Bitcoin and Ethereum before, during, and after major international football tournaments using monthly data from May 2016 to December 2025. Tournament-specific OLS regressions with market controls, calendar and trend adjustments, Newey–West HAC inference, and Benjamini-Hochberg FDR correction were employed in this study. The results show substantial heterogeneity across tournaments, cryptocurrencies, and temporal phases. Although several tournament-window coefficients are nominally significant, broader FDR adjustment substantially narrows the supported findings. The strongest evidence is concentrated around the FIFA World Cup, with a negative association between the tournament period and Bitcoin returns, and a negative associations between the pre-tournament periods and Ethereum returns. International football tournament periods are not uniformly associated with cryptocurrency returns, and the observed patterns depend on the specific asset, tournament phase, and event context. These findings are consistent with a potential behavioral or investor-attention mechanism, although attention and sentiment are not directly measured.

Methodology documentation for the Machine Economy Index (MEI) and its legal component, the Legal Rail Readiness Score (LRRS), methodology version 1.3 (20 October 2026). The record contains the conceptual and mathematical specification of the index framework, inclusion criteria, normalization bounds methodology, legal-coverage scoring rules, and versioning protocol. The MEI tracks machine-economy activity across payments, physical infrastructure, and legal readiness, plus a macro demand component, combined with a geometric mean. The LRRS measures GDP-weighted legal coverage across 48 jurisdictions and five categories. The public methodology overview is available at https://machineeconomy.ai/methodology. ## 26. [Conditional Integration of AI Equities with Traditional and Alternative Assets: Evidence from Wavelet Coherence and Time-Varying Connectedness](<https://doi.org/10.3390/jrfm19110907>)

- 作者：Heni Boubaker; Nadia Farjallah
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/jrfm19110907

**摘要：**

This study examines whether the equities of leading artificial intelligence (AI) firms were integrated with Bitcoin, gold, West Texas Intermediate (WTI) crude oil and clean energy from January 2018 to July 2026. Using an equally weighted index of NVIDIA, Apple, Alphabet and Microsoft, we combine wavelet coherence, time-varying parameter vector autoregressive (TVP-VAR) connectedness and time-varying Granger causality, complemented by windowed scalogram difference and variance decomposition analyses. Dependence is time-varying and horizon-dependent: significant co-movements concentrate around the COVID-19 crash, the 2022 inflation and monetary tightening shock, and the AI-driven boom. Average total connectedness was low (20.23%) but exceeded 50% in March 2020. Clean energy and the AI index are net transmitters of shocks, whereas Bitcoin, gold and oil are net receivers; however, much of the transmitting role of AI equities reflects their exposure to the broad equity market. Lagged predictability is episodic, and AI equities are almost never predicted by the other assets. The results are robust to ex ante AI exchange-traded funds, alternative model specifications, weekly data and Brent oil. AI equities therefore appear only conditionally integrated with other asset classes, and diversification benefits observed in calm periods should not be assumed to persist during stress episodes.

## 28. [РЕГРЕСІЙНО-СЦЕНАРНЕ МОДЕЛЮВАННЯ ДОВГОСТРОКОВОЇ ДИНАМІКИ КАПІТАЛІЗАЦІЇ СВІТОВОГО КРИПТОВАЛЮТНОГО РИНКУ: МЕТОДОЛОГІЧНІ ОБМЕЖЕННЯ ЕКСТРАПОЛЯЦІЙНИХ ПІДХОДІВ](<https://doi.org/10.32782/2522-1256-2026-50-18>)

- 作者：Mariana Myronova; Юлія Полякова
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.32782/2522-1256-2026-50-18
- [打开 PDF](<https://journals-lute.lviv.ua/index.php/pidpr-torgi/article/download/2495/2338>)

**摘要：**

У статті досліджено методологічні можливості й обмеження регресійно-екстраполяційного підходу до прогнозування довгострокової динаміки капіталізації світового криптовалютного ринку. Актуальність теми зумовлена стрімким і водночас вкрай нерівномірним зростанням крипторинку упродовж 2011-2025 рр., що ускладнює застосування традиційних трендових моделей для обґрунтування прогнозних і сценарних оцінок, необхідних суб’єктам зовнішньоекономічної діяльності та органам державної політики. На основі статистичного ряду капіталізації крипторинку за 2011-2025 рр., сформованого за даними провідних міжнародних аналітичних платформ, побудовано логарифмічно-лінійну регресійну модель методом найменших квадратів, отримано статистично значуще рівняння з коефіцієнтом детермінації 0,866 та значущим на рівні 1% коефіцієнтом нахилу. Емпірично доведено, що механічна екстраполяція моделі, побудованої на довгому ряді з фазою вибухового зростання від низької бази, генерує вкрай широкі довірчі інтервали прогнозу, які на практиці роблять точкові оцінки на кілька років уперед статистично необґрунтованими, а подекуди економічно абсурдними. Порівняльний аналіз регресії на повній вибірці (2011-2025 рр.) і скороченій вибірці зрілого ринку (2016-2025 рр.) підтвердив гіпотезу про уповільнення темпів зростання капіталізації в міру дозрівання ринку, що узгоджується з логікою S-подібної траєкторії дифузії фінансових інновацій та зростанням довіри учасників ринку в міру накопичення практичного досвіду використання криптоактивів. Обґрунтовано доцільність доповнення регресійного інструментарію сценарним підходом, що враховує альтернативні комбінації макроекономічних, регуляторних і технологічних факторів невизначеності та спирається на систематизацію актуальних прогнозів провідних фінансових інституцій світу. Результати дослідження мають практичне значення для формування адаптивних, а не точкових стратегій суб’єктів зовнішньоекономічної діяльності та регуляторної політики за умов високої волатильності криптовалютного ринку.

## 29. [Machine Learning for Money Laundering Analysis](<https://doi.org/10.53941/ijndi.2026.100024>)

- 作者：Victor I. C. Chang; Hai Wang; Siddharth Raut; Assel Akzhalova; Weihang Huang
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.53941/ijndi.2026.100024
- [打开 PDF](<https://media.sciltp.com/articles/2611005618/2611005618.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This study investigates the application of machine learning (ML) for proactive threat hunting in financial transaction systems, focusing on detecting money laundering activities—a critical challenge in business cybersecurity. Utilizing the Elliptic Bitcoin dataset, we apply six supervised ML models: Random Forest, Support Vector Machine, K-Nearest Neighbors, Stochastic Gradient Descent, Decision Tree, and Logistic Regression. We address class imbalance using the Synthetic Minority Oversampling Technique (SMOTE) and conduct exploratory data analysis to identify key transactional features. Evaluated using cybersecurity-relevant metrics (accuracy, precision, recall, F1-score), the Random Forest classifier outperforms others, achieving 98.87% accuracy on the holdout set. This work contributes an empirical benchmarking study demonstrating that, on the Elliptic Bitcoin dataset, Random Forest achieves the highest macro-F1 (0.965) and ROC-AUC (0.996) under random-split evaluation, while also revealing substantial performance degradation for all classifiers under temporally ordered evaluation—a finding with direct implications for realistic AML deployment.

---

## 30. [Post-trade Exposure Dispersion: Participant Composition and Calendar Weighting in a Transparent Perpetual Market (Companion Code and Aggregate Results)](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24086044>)

- 作者：Bolang Fang
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24086044

**摘要：**

This release contains companion code and aggregate results for a study of post-trade exposure dispersion in the Hyperliquid BTC perpetual market. The study follows the passive recipient addresses in each trade episode and measures changes in their signed BTC exposure over the next 30 seconds. The evaluation covers 48,636 source-supported episodes across 14 dates in December 2025, using selected UTC hours. The package includes Python scripts for verifying the saved results, a source-file inventory, and research implementation files. Run python -B verify.py from the repository root to verify the distributed materials. The raw trade files and private event-level ledgers are not included. The original data are available from Albers et al. at https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18184441.

## 31. [Bitcoin: The Little Rule That Beats the Market](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24092036>)

- 作者：Manuel Amon Bossi
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24092036

**摘要：**

I study a daily rule that sets Bitcoin exposure from two observable inputs: the trend of a foreign-currency basket against the dollar and the deviation of Bitcoin's price from a fixed power-law curve. I construct the basket from the Japanese yen, Chinese yuan and British pound, using Federal Reserve trade weights published at each annual reset. In this paper, the market means Bitcoin buy-and-hold. From January 1, 2014 to November 6, 2026, the rule earned a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 70.41%, a Sharpe ratio of 1.46, a maximum drawdown of -51.91% and a final multiple of 941.64 times. Buy-and-hold earned 43.19%, 0.87, -83.62% and 100.63 times. The results are a historical evaluation. Paired 95% bootstrap intervals include zero for the CAGR and Sharpe gaps and exclude zero for the drawdown gap. I selected the currencies and parameters with knowledge of this sample, and the account uses synthetic short positions and omits financing costs. I motivate the rule with the hypothesis that easier global dollar conditions support portfolio demand for Bitcoin. The backtest leaves that channel unidentified, and the rule needs a prospective holdout.

## 32. [ЕВОЛЮЦІЯ ЗАКОНОДАВСТВА У СФЕРІ КРИПТОВАЛЮТИ: ІСТОРИЧНІ ПЕРЕДУМОВИ ТА ЕТИЧНІ АСПЕКТИ КРИМІНАЛІЗАЦІЇ](<https://doi.org/10.32999/ksu2307-8049/2026-4-16>)

- 作者：Марія Георгіївна Корабель; Tetіana A. Pavlenko; Микола Дмитрович Ждан
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.32999/ksu2307-8049/2026-4-16
- [打开 PDF](<https://lj.journal.kspu.edu/index.php/lj/article/download/551/499>)

**摘要：**

Мета. Метою дослідження є комплексний аналіз історичних передумов виникнення криптовалюти та еволюції нормативно-правового регулювання суспільних відносин у сфері її використання в Україні, а також визначення особливостей і меж кримінально-правової охорони криптовалюти та етичних засад її криміналізації. Особливу увагу приділено встановленню взаємозв’язку між формуванням правового статусу криптовалюти та можливістю визнання її предметом кримінальних правопорушень, а також визначенню допустимих меж кримінально-правового втручання у сферу цифрових активів. Методи. У процесі дослідження використано комплекс загальнонаукових і спеціально-юридичних методів. За допомогою історико-правового методу простежено основні етапи становлення криптовалюти та розвитку законодавства у відповідній сфері. Формально-юридичний метод застосовано для аналізу нормативно-правових положень, що визначають правову природу та статус криптовалюти і віртуальних активів. Системний метод дозволив розглянути кримінально-правову охорону віртуальних активів у взаємозв’язку з іншими напрямами правового регулювання. За допомогою методу аналізу та узагальнення визначено основні проблеми криміналізації суспільно небезпечних діянь, пов’язаних із використанням криптовалюти, та сформульовано відповідні висновки. Результати. Установлено, що розвиток криптовалюти як результат еволюції електронних платіжних систем, цифрових грошей та децентралізованих технологій відбувався значно швидше, ніж формування належної нормативно-правової бази. Проаналізовано еволюцію підходів до правового регулювання криптовалюти в Україні – від початкової правової невизначеності та спроби співвіднести Bitcoin із традиційними правовими категоріями до поступового нормативного визнання поняття «віртуальний актив». Обґрунтовано, що визначення правової природи криптовалюти має принципове значення для кримінального права, оскільки створює передумови для визначення можливості визнання її предметом кримінальних правопорушень. Встановлено, що криптовалюта може мати самостійне майнове значення та водночас використовуватися у механізмах учинення суспільно небезпечних діянь, зокрема шахрайства, незаконного заволодіння майном, легалізації незаконно одержаних активів та інших правопорушень. Досліджено етичні аспекти криміналізації та визначено основними орієнтирами суспільну небезпечність діяння, справедливість, пропорційність, правову визначеність і принцип ultima ratio. Висновки. Розвиток законодавства у сфері криптовалюти є закономірною реакцією держави на трансформацію суспільних відносин під впливом цифрових технологій. Водночас своєчасне нормативне реагування не повинно призводити до необґрунтованого розширення кримінально-правової заборони. Криміналізація має стосуватися не криптовалюти як технологічного чи майнового явища, а конкретних суспільно небезпечних діянь, пов’язаних із її використанням. Подальше вдосконалення кримінально-правової охорони віртуальних активів має здійснюватися відповідно до динаміки розвитку суспільних відносин із забезпеченням балансу між ефективним захистом майнових інтересів, протидією кримінальним правопорушенням та недопущенням надмірного втручання у правомірний обіг цифрових активів.

## 33. [A Systematic Literature Review of Blockchain and Distributed Ledger Technology for the Detection and Prevention of Phishing Attacks](<https://doi.org/10.64388/ire1724971>)

- 作者：Egbele Wilfred Odianosen; Temitope Olufunmi Atoyebi; Ridwan Kolapo; Prema A. Kirubakaran
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.64388/ire1724971
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.irejournals.com/formatedpaper/1724971.pdf>)

**摘要：**

This study systematically reviews blockchain and distributed ledger technology (DLT) for phishing detection and prevention. It also identifies two related research streams: the use of DLT as the infrastructure for anti-phishing intelligence and analytical methods for identifying phishing in blockchain and Web3. The review synthesizes 45 publications through 2026 using PRISMA and reports descriptive mapping, thematic analysis, and evidence identification. Some of the functions of the ledger proposed include decentralized URL blacklists and auditable threat intelligence. In blockchain ecosystems, the dominant analytical methods include machine learning on account and transaction data, analysis of transaction graphs and other temporal graph models, contrastive learning, cross-chain adaptation, and payload inspection. The studies reviewed are largely focused on the Ethereum blockchain from 2023 to 2025. Other limitations identified in the studies include imbalanced classes, ambiguous or absence of labels, lack of both internal and external validation, and privacy and governance concerns. The report suggests the protective measures proposed may not substantially prevent financial loss. It concludes that, despite its potential for improving provability and accountability, the actual effect on phishing is limited without evidence of protective measures, and a timely, off-chain corrective and dispute process.

## 35. [Honeypot Buyer: An Inside Look at the Coordinated Inauthentic Behaviour Economy](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24120753>)

- 作者：Mike Wright
- 发表日期：2026-11-09
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24120753

**摘要：**

Most research on coordinated inauthentic behaviour (CIB) studies networks after theyhave been detected: takedown datasets, leaked tasking documents and platform reports. Less isknown about the commercial edge where CIB is sold, one customer at a time, to small businesses.This paper reports a fourteen-day engagement in which the author, operating a small investigativeFacebook page, accepted an unsolicited offer of reviews, likes and follows and played the vendor'sbuyer. The buyer was a honeypot: operator-only contact, no payment, a decoy target the operatorcontrolled, and a stopping rule. In that time the vendor delivered a sample review through aseparate persona account, quoted a price curve from USD 3 to USD 0.50 per review, offered fivepayment rails and disclosed two of them, a reused Bitcoin address and a UK e-money account,stated the account's registered holder in response to a screenshot pretext, and finally asked thebuyer to create and hand over a new Facebook page or account. We describe the vendor's tactics,techniques and procedures (TTPs) and map them to the DISARM framework. We analyse the socialengineering on both sides through persuasion and negotiation psychology, and release the pretextartifact as an open-source tool. The case shows that the buyer role is a productive collectionposition. It yields delivery, pricing, payment and asset-acquisition indicators that passiveobservation cannot reach, and it shows the retail CIB economy recruiting its own customers as asource of accounts.

## 36. [Do Investors Sanction Greenwashing? Selective Market Discipline in Fintech Firms](<https://doi.org/10.1002/bse.71781>)

- 作者：Shaen Corbet; José Antonio Muñiz; David Staunton
- 发表日期：2026-11-08
- 引用次数：2
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1002/bse.71781
- [打开 PDF](<https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdfdirect/10.1002/bse.71781>)

**摘要：**

ABSTRACT We investigate whether stock market investors detect and penalise greenwashing. We draw on media reports of environmental, social and governance incidents at 321 US‐listed fintech firms between 2007 and 2025, within which greenwashing is identified, following RepRisk, as misleading communication about environmental practice. Investors impose no measurable price penalty when firms are publicly implicated in greenwashing, and the sample is large enough to have detected a penalty of the size observed for other conduct. Misleading communication that does not concern the environment is penalised, where cumulative abnormal returns are between 0.4% and 0.6% lower over the 2 weeks following the report, falling further to 0.8% over the 4 weeks before and after, whereas physical environmental damage draws no adverse reaction. The market therefore prices dishonesty, but the sanction depends on the domain of the false claim, and environmental claims by fintech firms fall outside it. Consistent with stakeholder theory, the results suggest that market‐based discipline exists but is selective, so that the truthfulness of environmental claims cannot be presumed to be enforced by investors where regulatory oversight is absent.

## 37. [Safety in numbers: Tackling the deliberative, performative, and infrastructural roots of multi‐agent misalignment](<https://doi.org/10.1002/aaai.70124>)

- 作者：Jeffrey M. Bradshaw; Matthew Johnson; Niranjan Suri; Alessandro Amato; Kristen Brent Venable; Rebecca Montanari; Nicolò Romandini; Roberto Fronteddu; Larry Bunch; James L. Lott; William de Beaumont; Andrzej Uszok; Sara Cavicchi; Mica Vignati; Ben Shinnick; Brian A. Jalaian; Jane Pinelis; Michael T. Goodrich; Jurriaan van Diggelen; David D. Woods; Patrick J. Hayes; Margaret Warren; Manuele Tele Junior Fernandez
- 发表日期：2026-11-08
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1002/aaai.70124
- [打开 PDF](<https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdfdirect/10.1002/aaai.70124>)

**摘要：**

Abstract Multi‐agent systems (MAS)—a uniquely important subset of the wider field commonly called “Agentic AI”—enable complex work processes involving distributed networks of multiple AIs, people, and technology tools. However, they also carry big risks. Root causes of catastrophic safety failures in multi‐agent collaboration (as in human collaboration) can be classed into three overlapping categories: 1. Deliberative —deviating from shared MAS commitments to fulfill explicitly expressed joint intentions; 2. Performative —inability to coordinate well or to adapt to changing circumstances; and 3. Infrastructural —safety, reliability, and trust in the operating environment. In the spirit of the international Multi‐Agent System Safety and Teamwork (MASST) initiative (Bradshaw et al., 2026), our multi‐disciplinary research team seeks to tackle the root causes of these three challenges through development, experimentation, and deployment of an integrated multi‐layered safety pipeline, enabling “safety in numbers” as increasingly powerful multi‐agent systems are deployed. Although this proposal is merely an initial sketch, it is our hope that sharing these ideas will inspire others to contribute to the discussion as we continue moving forward. We look forward not only to exciting discoveries within laboratory settings while also being committed to improving safe rollout of large‐scale MAS into the world to perform work in high‐consequence applications.

---

## 38. [Governance of Hybrid Fintech Ecosystem: An Examination Using the Generativity‐Control Paradigm](<https://doi.org/10.1111/isj.70068>)

- 作者：Deepak Chandrashekar; Krishna Satyanarayana; Dr. Anuradha Sharma; Ramesh Srinivasan
- 发表日期：2026-11-08
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1111/isj.70068
- [打开 PDF](<https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/pdfdirect/10.1111/isj.70068>)

**摘要：**

ABSTRACT Digital platforms are increasingly leveraged to address social challenges in the financial sector. However, the fintech sector, particularly in developing economies, is inherently hybrid, creating distinct governance challenges. This study examined the applicability of generativity‐control governance literature to managing dual motives, a federated structure, and a trust‐based value orientation in hybrid fintech ecosystems. Drawing on the digital platform ecosystem governance literature as the theoretical foundation and the concept of holons from information ecology theory as the analytical lens, the study analysed interview data from 41 respondents representing the regulator, ecosystem orchestrator, fintech startups, financial institutions, incubators, and media in the Indian fintech ecosystem. It employed deductive reflexive thematic analysis to analyse the interview data and used secondary data to corroborate the findings. Accordingly, this study conceptualised complementors as holons exhibiting part‐whole behaviour, offering a novel theoretical explanation of how they maintain the generativity‐control balance and address governance challenges to achieve both economic and social outcomes simultaneously in hybrid fintech ecosystems. It also identified and defined three complementary governance mechanisms (demarcation, regulation, and legitimization) that address governance challenges associated with the ecosystem's motives, structure, and value orientation, respectively. Ultimately, the findings extend the information systems governance literature and contribute to the fintech literature by explaining how complementors' collaborative participation, their use of digital public infrastructure, and the provision of regulatory sandboxes within the Indian fintech ecosystem facilitate the generativity‐control balance.

## 39. [NeuPortal Forecast Ledger: Bitcoin-timestamped AI forecasts scored against market prices](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24051884>)

- 发表日期：2026-11-08
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24051884

**摘要：**

Each forecast in this dataset was timestamped into Bitcoin with OpenTimestamps, then scored against market prices with the Brier score. Each JSON file is the original stamped artifact, byte for byte, next to its .ots proof. Note: the first batch (14-26 September 2026, 1,613 forecasts) was sealed late, on 26 September, when 1,394 of them had already resolved; those are not evidence of forecasting in advance. Since 26 September every forecast is sealed minutes after it is made. As of 7 November 2026: 7,294 forecasts, 6,957 resolved. Brier score: ours 0.1936, the market's 0.1605 (lower is better), so the market is ahead. Tracks: weather, crypto, sports, markets, politics, tech. Known gaps are listed in the README: newest files may carry pending proofs, one file was never stamped, and a duplicate set sealed by mistake is kept but not scored. Verify any file at opentimestamps.org (drop the .ots first, then the .json) or with the opentimestamps-client.

## 41. [Economic Scarcity as Value: A Universal Framework for Blockchain and Beyond: paper and replication package](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24051776>)

- 作者：Agisilaos Papadogiannis
- 发表日期：2026-11-08
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24051776

**摘要：**

Working paper and full replication package. A structural valuation framework in which economic scarcity, the monetizable intersection of desirability and constrained access, is the foundation of asset value. Five protocol-level elements (Purpose, Incentives, Consensus, Tokenomics, Network Effects) are mapped to a network value function V = D S (u/u0)^alpha, with a scored physical-scarcity index S, normalized adoption u, and an implied desirability D recovered as the residual of market value. Applied to Bitcoin, Ethereum and Bittensor as of 5 August 2025: Bitcoin and Ethereum carry nearly identical implied desirability per normalized user, while Bittensor's valuation is expectation-dominated. Includes all data, scripts, result tables, figures and tests; the scripts reproduce every number in the paper.

## 43. [Graph-Based Adaptive Fusion Network for Multi-Horizon Cryptocurrency Price Forecasting](<https://doi.org/10.3390/math14214099>)

- 作者：Jantima Polpinij; Anirut Chotthanom; Theeraya Uttha; Woraphot Watthusin; Panu Tansorajprasert; Bungon Kumphon
- 发表日期：2026-11-08
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.3390/math14214099

**摘要：**

This study presents a Graph-Based Adaptive Fusion Network (GAFN) for forecasting the closing prices of Bitcoin, Ethereum, Litecoin, and XRP at 7-, 14-, and 30-day horizons. GAFN combines a BiLSTM encoder, a graph attention network constructed from training-period return correlations, and target-conditioned adaptive fusion. It predicts standardized future log returns as intermediate outputs, which are converted to closing-price forecasts for evaluation. The experiments used 3131 aligned daily observations from Yahoo Finance covering 9 November 2017 to 5 June 2026. The data were divided chronologically into training, validation, and confirmatory test partitions. The final 156 observations, covering 1 January to 5 June 2026, yielded 97 forecast origins after sequence generation. Feature and target standardization and graph construction used the training partition only; model selection and residual scaling used the validation partition. GAFN was compared with Persistence, recurrent, fusion, attention-based, and graph-based models using asset-level NRMSE and an unweighted macro-average across the four assets. Each trainable model was evaluated over five random seeds. Several simpler models achieved lower macro-averaged NRMSE than GAFN at 7 days. At 14 days, GAFN tied with LSTM at the reported precision. At the 30-day horizon, GAFN achieved a macro-averaged test NRMSE of 0.03997 ± 0.00019, compared with 0.03979 for Persistence and 0.04062 for Concatenation Fusion. GAFN had the lowest mean NRMSE among the trainable models at this horizon, but the observed differences were not statistically significant after adjustment for multiple comparisons. The ablation results also showed no consistent improvement from any individual component across all three horizons. GAFN was therefore competitive in this experiment, but the results do not establish superiority beyond the tested assets and period.

## 45. [Rancang Bangun Sistem Pelacakan Rantai Pasok Hardware Terdistribusi Menggunakan Smart Contract Hyperledger BESU dan IPFS](<https://ipssj.com/index.php/ojs/article/view/2246>)

- 作者：Oriza Panduwinata, Arzella Meilatika, Latifah Isnaini, Nabila Oktora, Elok Dewi Yuliana, Rahma Deani
- 发表日期：2026-11-08
- 引用次数：0
- [打开 PDF](<https://ipssj.com/index.php/ojs/article/download/2246/2084>)

**摘要：**

Kerentanan rantai pasok perangkat keras (hardware supply chain) terhadap pemalsuan komponen elektronik, perubahan spesifikasi secara ilegal, dan manipulasi riwayat manufaktur menimbulkan ancaman serius bagi keandalan infrastruktur pusat data enterprise dan institusi publik. Sistem pencatatan logistik berbasis basis data relasional terpusat terbukti rentan terhadap manipulasi basis data oleh administrator internal dan kegagalan titik tunggal (single point of failure). Untuk mengatasi persoalan tersebut, penelitian ini merancang sistem pelacakan rantai pasok hardware terdistribusi yang memadukan kontrak pintar Solidity pada permissioned blockchain Hyperledger Besu dengan konsensus Quorum Byzantine Fault Tolerance (QBFT) dan penyimpanan off-chain terdesentralisasi InterPlanetary File System (IPFS). Sertifikat keaslian sirkuit terpadu, lembar pengujian pabrik, dan jejak transfer kepemilikan dienkripsi serta dipetakan ke dalam token non-fungible (NFT) standar ERC-721 yang terikat dengan nomor seri unik komponen. Mengadopsi Design Science Research Methodology (DSRM), pengujian empiris dijalankan secara ketat pada klaster server fisik tervirtualisasi Proxmox VE. Hasil pengujian menunjukkan throughput transaksi pencatatan mencapai 292 transaksi per detik (TPS), latensi konsensus rata-rata 1,38 detik pada beban puncak, dan overhead konsumsi gas sebesar 58.420 unit per perpindahan rantai pasok. Sistem ini menjamin keterlacakan end-to-end, integritas data tak terbantahkan, serta kekebalan terhadap serangan kolusi tanpa mengorbankan skalabilitas komputasi.

---

## 46. [Creating satisfaction and loyalty among tourists: the power of experiential tourism](<https://doi.org/10.1057/s41599-026-09380-5>)

- 作者：Shagun Chib; Sahil Singh Jasrotia; Surabhi Koul; Vijay Amrit Raj
- 发表日期：2026-11-07
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.1057/s41599-026-09380-5
- [打开 PDF](<https://www.nature.com/articles/s41599-026-09380-5_reference.pdf>)

**摘要：**

Abstract The current research examines whether experiential tourism activities in the tourism industry can influence tourists’ behaviour. Using the stimulus-organism-response framework, the study examines how experience affects travellers’ intentions for satisfaction and loyalty among online travel agents (OTAs). Data were collected from 208 visitors across five travel destinations in India, specifically wildlife sanctuaries and national parks, to examine their lived experiences. The results were analysed using a partial least squares structural equation modelling (PLS-SEM) approach, indicating that experiential tourism indicators have a direct effect on customer engagement. The study suggests that experiential motivation is vital in shaping tourists’ satisfaction and loyalty. The results suggest that tourists are more likely to return to the OTA and recommend it to others if they have positive experiential motivation during their travel encounter. Moreover, the study highlights the importance of co-creation in the tourism industry. In the challenging competitive space, leveraging experiential strategies can help managers gain a competitive advantage. Travel retailers must focus on increasing tourists’ conscious participation and include certain activities that may motivate them and create enthusiasm. This study is one of the few in India to explore the relationship between experiential activities and tourist satisfaction and loyalty among wildlife sanctuaries and national parks travellers.

## 48. [Public Expenditure Forecasting in Cambodia: A Hybrid Econometric and Machine Learning Approach](<https://doi.org/10.32479/ijefi.24858>)

- 作者：Tepwinuth Chhim; Siphat Lim
- 发表日期：2026-11-07
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.32479/ijefi.24858
- [打开 PDF](<https://econjournals.com/index.php/ijefi/article/download/24858/10530>)

**摘要：**

This study compared traditional time-series and machine-learning methods for the monthly forecasting of government expenditure of Cambodia with dataset. The study employed a natural logarithm of the monthly flows of government expenditure from January 2009 to March 2016. The dataset has 207 observations and was divided into train set, where the general model training took place (80%) and test set out of sample (20%). Mean Absolute Error (MAE), Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE) and Mean Absolute Percentage Error (MAPE) are the metrics that are used to evaluate forecasting performance. The study compares the benchmark against the Autoregressive Integrated Moving Average (ARIMA) and four machine-learning approaches: Hybrid ARIMA-Random Forest, Random Forest (RF), Extreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost) and Support Vector Regression (SVR). The ARIMA model detects a statistical significant seasonal autoregressive behaviour and a drift, indicating that government expenditure is characterized by long-run growth and seasonal dynamics. Nevertheless, the forecasting accuracy using the traditional method is outmatched by results of machine-learning models in general. In particular, XGBoost outperforms all the other models with the lowest MAE, RMSE and MAPE metrics indicating that it is the best forecasting technique. The Random Forest is in the second position while Hybrid ARIMA-Random Forest model significantly outperforms standalone ARIMA model, though it remains below the tree-based ensemble methods. SVR also beats ARIMA, but fails to beat XGBoost and Random Forest. Overall, the results illustrate that machine-learning approaches, particularly XGBoost and Random forest, consistently outperform the traditional ARIMA model in terms of fitting government expenditure´s nonlinear, seasonal and trend pattern, which would enhance forecasts accuracy for fiscal planning and policy decisions.

---

## 49. [Intermediação financeira 3.0: a reconfiguração dos custos de transação na era da tokenização](<https://doi.org/10.22456/2176-5456.152842>)

- 作者：Cristiano Aguiar de Oliveira; Cristiano Aguiar de Oliveira; Manoel Gustavo Neubarth Trindade
- 发表日期：2026-11-07
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.22456/2176-5456.152842
- [打开 PDF](<https://seer.ufrgs.br/index.php/AnaliseEconomica/article/download/152842/101667>)

**摘要：**

Este artigo analisa as promessas e os limites da tokenização à luz da Teoria dos Custos de Transação (TCT), com o objetivo de oferecer uma interpretação pragmática dos ganhos e das novas fricções geradas por essa tecnologia emergente. Argumenta-se que, embora a tokenização reduza diversos custos associados à intermediação tradicional — como liquidação, custódia e reconciliação —, ela também reconfigura os custos de mensuração, monitoramento e enforcement, deslocando-os do plano ex post para o ex ante. A partir dos conceitos clássicos de Coase, Williamson e North, demonstra-se que as principais disfunções observadas nos modelos tokenizados — incluindo rigidez contratual, captura de governança, fragmentação de liquidez e persistência de intermediários informacionais — são previsíveis em termos transacionais. Conclui-se que modelos baseados em tokenização, especialmente no âmbito das finanças descentralizadas (DeFi), representam uma transformação institucional promissora, sobretudo para países com instituições frágeis, ao permitir novos arranjos de confiança em contextos de insegurança jurídica. Propõe-se, por fim, uma agenda de pesquisa voltada à modelagem dos trade-offs institucionais e ao desenho de governanças adaptativas para infraestruturas descentralizadas.

---

## 50. [An Evidence-Preserving Migration Model for Post-Quantum Permissioned Supply-Chain Ledgers: Certificate Referencing and Checkpoint Anchoring](<https://doi.org/10.58257/ijprems55240>)

- 作者：未提供
- 发表日期：2026-11-07
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.58257/ijprems55240
- [打开 PDF](<https://doi.org/10.58257/ijprems55240>)

**摘要：**

Permissioned blockchains increasingly record provenance for medicines, aircraft parts and food, and many of those records must stay verifiable for decades.The signatures that bind them to their authors rest on RSA and elliptic-curve assumptions that a large fault-tolerant quantum computer would break.Existing work concentrates on which post-quantum algorithm to use and how large its keys and signatures are.Two practical questions for a live ledger receive less attention: how long each class of record can safely wait for migration, and how evidence signed classically can remain trustworthy after the classical scheme falls.This paper proposes an evidence-preserving migration framework for Hyperledger Fabric-style networks.Its elements are (i) a retention-aware risk model that ranks assets by migration slack, (ii) policy epochs that move the network through classical, hybrid and post-quantum verification modes by configuration transaction, with a hybrid signature that binds epoch and certificate reference to resist downgrade and substitution, (iii) an on-ledger certificate registry that lets a transaction carry a 32-byte digest instead of a full certificate, and (iv) periodic post-quantum-signed Merkle checkpoints that anchor historical blocks.Using only published parameter sizes, we derive an analytical model: for ML-DSA-65 in hybrid mode, certificate referencing lowers modelled signature-related bytes per transaction from about 27.5 KB to about 10.2 KB (a 63% reduction) for a client and two endorsers, while checkpoint storage is negligible by comparison.An evaluation protocol is specified.This paper reports the design, the analytical model and the evaluation methodology; empirical measurements are reserved for the implementation phase and are not claimed here.

- 发表日期：2026-11-07
- 引用次数：0

**摘要：**

Governance core and experiment code for the FinTrust paper. FinTrust sits between several probabilistic forecasters and a trading decision: it scores each forecaster on matured outcomes, moves it through an explicit lifecycle (shadow, active, probation, quarantine) and records every decision in a hash-chained audit log. The release regenerates every table and figure of the paper from saved model outputs (FI-2010, Bitstamp BTC/USD and NSE daily forecasts), and the forecasts themselves from public data. Raw market data are not redistributed; download scripts are included. No trading-profit claim is made.

## 53. [The 2026 Global Liquidation of Fraud Hypothesis: Sovereign Seizure of Shadow Bitcoin Reserves, Thermodynamic Collapse of AI Scaling Hype, and Deterministic Settlement via QFS-zkVM](<https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24024873>)

- 作者：Yoko(洋子) Hasebe(長谷部)
- 发表日期：2026-11-07
- 引用次数：0
- DOI：https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.24024873

**摘要：**

This monograph formalizes the "2026 Global Liquidation of Fraud" (世界規模の不正清算) hypothesis proposed by Yoko Hasebe, demonstrating the structural isomorphism between two seemingly disparate macro-events in early 2026: (1) the kinetic seizure of the Maduro regime's estimated 600,000 BTC "Shadow Reserve" following Operation Absolute Resolve and its prospective absorption into the U.S. Strategic Bitcoin Reserve (SBR) under Executive Order 14233, and (2) the institutional authority contraction within frontier AI laboratories (exemplified by Google DeepMind) driven by physical high-bandwidth memory (HBM) bottlenecks, grid power deficits, data exhaustion, and negative return-on-investment (ROI) scaling walls. We construct a unified thermodynamic and algebraic model under RTO P2.0 and QFS-zkVM over the 64-bit Goldilocks prime field ($\\mathbb{F}\_p, p = 2^{64}-2^{32}+1$) that distinguishes unbacked paper claims and illicit shadow liquidity from verifiable Proof-of-Work and physical infrastructure assets. Furthermore, we integrate Q-SAFA cryptographic sovereign asset forfeiture auditing, PHOENIX\_BRIDGE / PHOENIX\_ROUTER liquidity isolation gates, Human-in-Command (HIC) fiduciary interlocks, SEC Rule 15c3-5 pre-trade market access controls, and Delaware Caremark board oversight duties to establish a rigorous institutional framework for surviving systemic asset impairment and sovereign supply shocks.
